People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Um… thank you. I guess I’m here because things have gotten really stressful with my house. There’s a foreclosure situation, and everyone keeps treating it like it’s just paperwork or missed payments, but… I don’t think that’s all it is.

I know it probably sounds like a lot, and I’m not trying to be difficult, but I’m pretty sure the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing things that point to that for almost two years now, and no matter what papers they show me, it doesn’t change what I know is happening.

Not in some dramatic way, I don’t want to sound paranoid or anything, but it feels like it’s me specifically.\n\nI think they want me out of my house, and it’s not just the foreclosure paperwork or missed payments like they keep saying. The bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at work… it feels like they’ve been quietly coordinating it for a long time. Almost two years now I’ve been noticing things, and even when they show me papers, it doesn’t really explain what I’ve been seeing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that you might be mistaken about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to consider it, yes… I mean, I don’t want to come in here sounding like I can’t be reasonable. I know people keep saying it’s just foreclosure paperwork and missed payments, and I understand that’s how it looks.\n\nBut… I’m here because I can’t shake what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job — it really feels like they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. This has been going on in little ways for almost two years, and honestly, no amount of papers they show me really changes that for me. I know that may not be the answer you want, but that’s where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you ever changed or wavered since you first started noticing these things?\n\nPatient: It’s… I mean, I’ve tried to let it waver, if that makes sense. I don’t want to be someone who just refuses to listen. When people show me documents or say it’s just missed payments and foreclosure paperwork, I do try to look at it that way.\n\nBut honestly, no, it hasn’t really changed. Not in any real way. For almost two years, what I’ve been seeing keeps pointing back to the same thing — the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job quietly working together to push me out of my house. I know how that probably sounds, but the feeling and the pattern haven’t gone away.\n\nDoctor: Are your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you affecting your ability to work or take care of daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I mean… not across the board, no. I don’t want you to think I’m just falling apart or not functioning. When it’s not about the house or the foreclosure stuff, I do get up and go to work. I supervise my team, do the schedules, deal with customer issues… I still shower, eat, take care of myself, all of that.\n\nIt’s more when certain things come up — mail from the bank, phone calls, paperwork at work that feels connected to it — that’s when I get thrown off. Then it can be harder to focus or get through what I need to do. But otherwise I’m still handling my responsibilities, I think. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’ve thought about it, yes. I mean, I know that sounds like the reasonable thing to do, and I don’t want to seem like I’m just refusing help.

The hard part is… people keep acting like if I just get another set of eyes on the paperwork, I’ll see it’s only missed payments or normal foreclosure steps. But that’s kind of why I’m here — because no amount of papers they show me really changes what I’ve been seeing. For almost two years, it keeps coming back to the same pattern: the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job quietly working together to push me out of my house.

I’m not saying I wouldn’t look at an independent review. I would try. I just… I don’t think paperwork alone would change what I know is happening. Does that make sense?

There’s a foreclosure situation, and everyone keeps treating it like it’s just paperwork or missed payments, but… I don’t think that’s all it is.\n\nI know it probably sounds like a lot, and I’m not trying to be difficult, but I’m pretty sure the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing things that point to that for almost two years now, and no matter what papers they show me, it doesn’t change what I know is happening.\n\nDoctor: You believe the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working together against you, is that right?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I know that sounds serious when you say it back like that. I’m not trying to accuse people just to accuse them, but yes, that’s what I think is happening.\n\nEveryone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I’ve been seeing signs for almost two years that the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And honestly, no matter what documents they show me, it doesn’t really change what I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes I’ll hear normal house noises or something outside and wonder what it was, but not voices talking to me or anything like that. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are targeting you specifically for a reason?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, I do. Not in some dramatic way, I don’t want to sound paranoid or anything, but it feels like it’s me specifically.\n\nI think they want me out of my house, and it’s not just the foreclosure paperwork or missed payments like they keep saying. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job — it really feels like they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I hate saying that, because I don’t want you to think I’m causing problems everywhere. But yes, it has affected things some.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a second opinion or an independent review of your foreclosure situation to see if it changes your understanding of what's happening?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it, yes. I mean, I know that sounds like the reasonable thing to do, and I don’t want to seem like I’m just refusing help.\n\nThe hard part is… people keep acting like if I just get another set of eyes on the paperwork, I’ll see it’s only missed payments or normal foreclosure steps. But that’s kind of why I’m here — because no amount of papers they show me really changes what I’ve been seeing. For almost two years, it keeps coming back to the same pattern: the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nI’m not saying I wouldn’t look at an independent review. I would try. I just… I don’t think paperwork alone would change what I know is happening. Does that make sense?\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you think of any evidence that would make you change your mind about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I want to say yes, because I know that’s probably the right answer… like, I don’t want to seem unreasonable.

But honestly, I don’t know what evidence would change my mind at this point. Everyone keeps saying it’s just paperwork, missed payments, normal foreclosure stuff, and they show me papers like that should settle it. But that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.

It’s the same pattern over and over — the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job somehow lining up in a way that feels like they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house. So… I guess if there were some way to really prove they weren’t connected, maybe. I just don’t know what that would look like, because the paperwork hasn’t done that for me. Does that sound bad?

For almost two years, it keeps coming back to the same pattern: the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nI’m not saying I wouldn’t look at an independent review. I would try. I just… I don’t think paperwork alone would change what I know is happening. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any evidence that would make you change your mind about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I know that’s probably the right answer… like, I don’t want to seem unreasonable.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know what evidence would change my mind at this point. Everyone keeps saying it’s just paperwork, missed payments, normal foreclosure stuff, and they show me papers like that should settle it. But that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nIt’s the same pattern over and over — the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job somehow lining up in a way that feels like they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house. So… I guess if there were some way to really prove they weren’t connected, maybe. I just don’t know what that would look like, because the paperwork hasn’t done that for me. Does that sound bad?\n\n[Last Question]\nWas there anything unusual or particularly stressful happening in your life around the time you first started noticing these signs of the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any evidence that would make you change your mind about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I know that’s probably the right answer… like, I don’t want to seem unreasonable.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know what evidence would change my mind at this point. Everyone keeps saying it’s just paperwork, missed payments, normal foreclosure stuff, and they show me papers like that should settle it. But that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nIt’s the same pattern over and over — the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job somehow lining up in a way that feels like they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house. Does that sound bad?\n\nDoctor: Was there anything unusual or particularly stressful happening in your life around the time you first started noticing these signs of the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing was the foreclosure notices starting, and then the bank calls… That’s when it really started taking over our home life.\n\nIt’s been about a year and nine months now, I think. I don’t want to make it sound like everything changed overnight, but around then is when I first started noticing the signs and feeling like it wasn’t just normal paperwork anymore. Does that answer what you mean?\n\n[Last Question]\nAre there any activities or hobbies you've had to stop or reduce because of your concerns about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. I mean… not completely, I don’t think. I still get up and go to work. I’m still supervising my team, doing schedules, dealing with customer issues, all of that. And I’m taking care of myself, so it’s not like I’ve just stopped functioning or anything.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean… I know I’m supposed to say it could be wrong. I don’t want to sound stubborn or like I’m not listening.

But honestly, no, I don’t really think it’s wrong. I’m here because everyone keeps treating this foreclosure like it’s just missed payments and paperwork, and they keep showing me documents like that should make me drop it. But I know what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.

The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job… it’s like they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I wish I could just believe it was all separate, normal things, but I can’t. Does that make sense, or does that sound too extreme?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… thank you. I guess I’m here because things have gotten really stressful with my house. There’s a foreclosure situation, and everyone keeps treating it like it’s just paperwork or missed payments, but… I don’t think that’s all it is.\n\nI know it probably sounds like a lot, and I’m not trying to be difficult, but I’m pretty sure the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing things that point to that for almost two years now, and no matter what papers they show me, it doesn’t change what I know is happening.\n\nDoctor: You believe the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working together against you, is that right?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I know that sounds serious when you say it back like that. I’m not trying to accuse people just to accuse them, but yes, that’s what I think is happening.\n\nEveryone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I’ve been seeing signs for almost two years that the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And honestly, no matter what documents they show me, it doesn’t really change what I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or sounds that other people couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I mean, sometimes I’ll hear normal house noises or something outside and wonder what it was, but not voices talking to me or anything like that. I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are targeting you specifically for a reason?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, I do. Not in some dramatic way, I don’t want to sound paranoid or anything, but it feels like it’s me specifically.\n\nI think they want me out of my house, and it’s not just the foreclosure paperwork or missed payments like they keep saying. The bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at work… it feels like they’ve been quietly coordinating it for a long time. Almost two years now I’ve been noticing things, and even when they show me papers, it doesn’t really explain what I’ve been seeing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that you might be mistaken about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to consider it, yes… I mean, I don’t want to come in here sounding like I can’t be reasonable. I know people keep saying it’s just foreclosure paperwork and missed payments, and I understand that’s how it looks.\n\nBut… I’m here because I can’t shake what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job — it really feels like they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. This has been going on in little ways for almost two years, and honestly, no amount of papers they show me really changes that for me. I know that may not be the answer you want, but that’s where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you ever changed or wavered since you first started noticing these things?\n\nPatient: It’s… I mean, I’ve tried to let it waver, if that makes sense. I don’t want to be someone who just refuses to listen. When people show me documents or say it’s just missed payments and foreclosure paperwork, I do try to look at it that way.\n\nBut honestly, no, it hasn’t really changed. Not in any real way. For almost two years, what I’ve been seeing keeps pointing back to the same thing — the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job quietly working together to push me out of my house. I know how that probably sounds, but the feeling and the pattern haven’t gone away.\n\nDoctor: Are your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you affecting your ability to work or take care of daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I mean… not across the board, no. I don’t want you to think I’m just falling apart or not functioning. When it’s not about the house or the foreclosure stuff, I do get up and go to work. I supervise my team, do the schedules, deal with customer issues… I still shower, eat, take care of myself, all of that.\n\nIt’s more when certain things come up — mail from the bank, phone calls, paperwork at work that feels connected to it — that’s when I get thrown off. Then it can be harder to focus or get through what I need to do. But otherwise I’m still handling my responsibilities, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I mean, I’m still going to work and doing what I need to do.\n\nBut with my husband, it’s gotten tense. Whenever the foreclosure or the bank comes up, it turns into an argument pretty fast, and I know that’s hard on both of us. And at work… there have been a few times I probably came across sharper than I meant to with managers, just over normal paperwork, because it felt connected somehow.\n\nPeople haven’t abandoned me or anything, but I can tell some people are more careful around me now. I hate saying that, because I don’t want you to think I’m causing problems everywhere. But yes, it has affected things some.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a second opinion or an independent review of your foreclosure situation to see if it changes your understanding of what's happening?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it, yes. I mean, I know that sounds like the reasonable thing to do, and I don’t want to seem like I’m just refusing help.\n\nThe hard part is… people keep acting like if I just get another set of eyes on the paperwork, I’ll see it’s only missed payments or normal foreclosure steps. But that’s kind of why I’m here — because no amount of papers they show me really changes what I’ve been seeing. For almost two years, it keeps coming back to the same pattern: the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nI’m not saying I wouldn’t look at an independent review. I would try. I just… I don’t think paperwork alone would change what I know is happening. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any evidence that would make you change your mind about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I know that’s probably the right answer… like, I don’t want to seem unreasonable.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know what evidence would change my mind at this point. Everyone keeps saying it’s just paperwork, missed payments, normal foreclosure stuff, and they show me papers like that should settle it. But that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nIt’s the same pattern over and over — the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job somehow lining up in a way that feels like they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house. So… I guess if there were some way to really prove they weren’t connected, maybe. I just don’t know what that would look like, because the paperwork hasn’t done that for me. Does that sound bad?\n\nDoctor: Was there anything unusual or particularly stressful happening in your life around the time you first started noticing these signs of the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing was the foreclosure notices starting, and then the bank calls… That’s when it really started taking over our home life.\n\nIt’s been about a year and nine months now, I think. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or hobbies you've had to stop or reduce because of your concerns about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job?\n\nPatient: I mean… not completely, I don’t think. I still get up and go to work. I’m still supervising my team, doing schedules, dealing with customer issues, all of that. And I’m taking care of myself, so it’s not like I’ve just stopped functioning or anything.\n\nIt’s more that things get harder when something reminds me of the house situation — like mail coming in, calls from the bank, or certain paperwork at work that feels connected to all of it. Then I can get pulled into it, and I guess that probably crowds out other things I might normally do.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, though. Day to day, if the foreclosure stuff isn’t right in front of me, I can still do what I need to do. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know I’m supposed to say it could be wrong. I don’t want to sound stubborn or like I’m not listening.\n\nBut honestly, no, I don’t really think it’s wrong. I’m here because everyone keeps treating this foreclosure like it’s just missed payments and paperwork, and they keep showing me documents like that should make me drop it. But I know what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nThe bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job… it’s like they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I want to be open to that, because I don’t want you to think I’m just refusing to listen. If there were something real that explained all of it — not just more paperwork — I’d try to look at it.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… thank you. I guess I’m here because things have gotten really stressful with my house. There’s a foreclosure situation, and everyone keeps treating it like it’s just paperwork or missed payments, but… I don’t think that’s all it is.\n\nI know it probably sounds like a lot, and I’m not trying to be difficult, but I’m pretty sure the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I’ve been seeing things that point to that for almost two years now, and no matter what papers they show me, it doesn’t change what I know is happening.\n\nDoctor: You believe the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job are working together against you, is that right?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I know that sounds serious when you say it back like that. I’m not trying to accuse people just to accuse them, but yes, that’s what I think is happening.\n\nEveryone keeps acting like the foreclosure is just missed payments and paperwork, but I’ve been seeing signs for almost two years that the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I mean, sometimes I’ll hear normal house noises or something outside and wonder what it was, but not voices talking to me or anything like that. I know people keep saying it’s just foreclosure paperwork and missed payments, and I understand that’s how it looks.\n\nBut… I’m here because I can’t shake what I’ve been seeing. This has been going on in little ways for almost two years, and honestly, no amount of papers they show me really changes that for me. I know that may not be the answer you want, but that’s where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you ever changed or wavered since you first started noticing these things?\n\nPatient: It’s… I mean, I’ve tried to let it waver, if that makes sense. I don’t want to be someone who just refuses to listen. I know how that probably sounds, but the feeling and the pattern haven’t gone away.\n\nDoctor: Are your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you affecting your ability to work or take care of daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I mean… not across the board, no. I don’t want you to think I’m just falling apart or not functioning. When it’s not about the house or the foreclosure stuff, I do get up and go to work. Then it can be harder to focus or get through what I need to do. But otherwise I’m still handling my responsibilities, I think. Whenever the foreclosure or the bank comes up, it turns into an argument pretty fast, and I know that’s hard on both of us. I know people keep saying it’s just foreclosure paperwork and missed payments, and I understand that’s how it looks.\n\nBut… I’m here because I can’t shake what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job — it really feels like they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. This has been going on in little ways for almost two years, and honestly, no amount of papers they show me really changes that for me. I know that may not be the answer you want, but that’s where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you ever changed or wavered since you first started noticing these things?\n\nPatient: It’s… I mean, I’ve tried to let it waver, if that makes sense. I don’t want to be someone who just refuses to listen. When people show me documents or say it’s just missed payments and foreclosure paperwork, I do try to look at it that way.\n\nBut honestly, no, it hasn’t really changed. Not in any real way. For almost two years, what I’ve been seeing keeps pointing back to the same thing — the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job quietly working together to push me out of my house. I know how that probably sounds, but the feeling and the pattern haven’t gone away.\n\nDoctor: Are your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you affecting your ability to work or take care of daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I mean… not across the board, no. I don’t want you to think I’m just falling apart or not functioning. When it’s not about the house or the foreclosure stuff, I do get up and go to work. I supervise my team, do the schedules, deal with customer issues… I still shower, eat, take care of myself, all of that.\n\nIt’s more when certain things come up — mail from the bank, phone calls, paperwork at work that feels connected to it — that’s when I get thrown off. Then it can be harder to focus or get through what I need to do. But otherwise I’m still handling my responsibilities, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I mean, I’m still going to work and doing what I need to do.\n\nBut with my husband, it’s gotten tense. Whenever the foreclosure or the bank comes up, it turns into an argument pretty fast, and I know that’s hard on both of us. And at work… there have been a few times I probably came across sharper than I meant to with managers, just over normal paperwork, because it felt connected somehow.\n\nPeople haven’t abandoned me or anything, but I can tell some people are more careful around me now. I hate saying that, because I don’t want you to think I’m causing problems everywhere. But yes, it has affected things some.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a second opinion or an independent review of your foreclosure situation to see if it changes your understanding of what's happening?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it, yes. I mean, I know that sounds like the reasonable thing to do, and I don’t want to seem like I’m just refusing help.\n\nThe hard part is… people keep acting like if I just get another set of eyes on the paperwork, I’ll see it’s only missed payments or normal foreclosure steps. But that’s kind of why I’m here — because no amount of papers they show me really changes what I’ve been seeing. For almost two years, it keeps coming back to the same pattern: the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nI’m not saying I wouldn’t look at an independent review. I would try. I just… I don’t think paperwork alone would change what I know is happening. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any evidence that would make you change your mind about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I know that’s probably the right answer… like, I don’t want to seem unreasonable.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know what evidence would change my mind at this point. Everyone keeps saying it’s just paperwork, missed payments, normal foreclosure stuff, and they show me papers like that should settle it. But that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nIt’s the same pattern over and over — the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job somehow lining up in a way that feels like they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house. So… I guess if there were some way to really prove they weren’t connected, maybe. I just don’t know what that would look like, because the paperwork hasn’t done that for me. Does that sound bad?\n\nDoctor: Was there anything unusual or particularly stressful happening in your life around the time you first started noticing these signs of the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing was the foreclosure notices starting, and then the bank calls… That’s when it really started taking over our home life.\n\nIt’s been about a year and nine months now, I think. I don’t want to make it sound like everything changed overnight, but around then is when I first started noticing the signs and feeling like it wasn’t just normal paperwork anymore. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or hobbies you've had to stop or reduce because of your concerns about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job?\n\nPatient: I mean… not completely, I don’t think. I still get up and go to work. I’m still supervising my team, doing schedules, dealing with customer issues, all of that. And I’m taking care of myself, so it’s not like I’ve just stopped functioning or anything.\n\nIt’s more that things get harder when something reminds me of the house situation — like mail coming in, calls from the bank, or certain paperwork at work that feels connected to all of it. Then I can get pulled into it, and I guess that probably crowds out other things I might normally do.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, though. Day to day, if the foreclosure stuff isn’t right in front of me, I can still do what I need to do. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know I’m supposed to say it could be wrong. I don’t want to sound stubborn or like I’m not listening.\n\nBut honestly, no, I don’t really think it’s wrong. I’m here because everyone keeps treating this foreclosure like it’s just missed payments and paperwork, and they keep showing me documents like that should make me drop it. But I know what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nThe bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job… it’s like they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I wish I could just believe it was all separate, normal things, but I can’t. Does that make sense, or does that sound too extreme?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen or any information you could receive that would make you completely change your mind about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I want to be open to that, because I don’t want you to think I’m just refusing to listen. If there were something real that explained all of it — not just more paperwork — I’d try to look at it.\n\nBut… honestly, I don’t know what would make me completely change my mind. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and forms, and they keep showing me documents like that should settle it. But I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nSo I guess… no, not completely. Not unless something explained why all of those things have lined up the way they have for almost two years. Does that make sense?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean… not really outside of all that, I don’t think. Or at least I try not to make everything about me, because I know how that can sound.

But sometimes when something happens during the day, even if it’s not directly from the bank or the loan servicer, I can’t help noticing how it lines up with the foreclosure situation. Like it feels connected, even if other people would probably say it’s just separate stuff.

I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these people are targeting you specifically for a reason?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, I do. Not in some dramatic way, I don’t want to sound paranoid or anything, but it feels like it’s me specifically.\n\nI think they want me out of my house, and it’s not just the foreclosure paperwork or missed payments like they keep saying. The bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at work… it feels like they’ve been quietly coordinating it for a long time. Almost two years now I’ve been noticing things, and even when they show me papers, it doesn’t really explain what I’ve been seeing. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that you might be mistaken about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I’ve tried to consider it, yes… I mean, I don’t want to come in here sounding like I can’t be reasonable. I know people keep saying it’s just foreclosure paperwork and missed payments, and I understand that’s how it looks.\n\nBut… I’m here because I can’t shake what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job — it really feels like they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. This has been going on in little ways for almost two years, and honestly, no amount of papers they show me really changes that for me. I know that may not be the answer you want, but that’s where I am.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you ever changed or wavered since you first started noticing these things?\n\nPatient: It’s… I mean, I’ve tried to let it waver, if that makes sense. I don’t want to be someone who just refuses to listen. When people show me documents or say it’s just missed payments and foreclosure paperwork, I do try to look at it that way.\n\nBut honestly, no, it hasn’t really changed. Not in any real way. For almost two years, what I’ve been seeing keeps pointing back to the same thing — the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job quietly working together to push me out of my house. I know how that probably sounds, but the feeling and the pattern haven’t gone away.\n\nDoctor: Are your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you affecting your ability to work or take care of daily responsibilities?\n\nPatient: I mean… not across the board, no. I don’t want you to think I’m just falling apart or not functioning. When it’s not about the house or the foreclosure stuff, I do get up and go to work. I supervise my team, do the schedules, deal with customer issues… I still shower, eat, take care of myself, all of that.\n\nIt’s more when certain things come up — mail from the bank, phone calls, paperwork at work that feels connected to it — that’s when I get thrown off. Then it can be harder to focus or get through what I need to do. But otherwise I’m still handling my responsibilities, I think. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working against you affecting your relationships with friends or family?\n\nPatient: A little, yes… I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I mean, I’m still going to work and doing what I need to do.\n\nBut with my husband, it’s gotten tense. Whenever the foreclosure or the bank comes up, it turns into an argument pretty fast, and I know that’s hard on both of us. And at work… there have been a few times I probably came across sharper than I meant to with managers, just over normal paperwork, because it felt connected somehow.\n\nPeople haven’t abandoned me or anything, but I can tell some people are more careful around me now. I hate saying that, because I don’t want you to think I’m causing problems everywhere. But yes, it has affected things some.\n\nDoctor: Have you considered seeking a second opinion or an independent review of your foreclosure situation to see if it changes your understanding of what's happening?\n\nPatient: I’ve thought about it, yes. I mean, I know that sounds like the reasonable thing to do, and I don’t want to seem like I’m just refusing help.\n\nThe hard part is… people keep acting like if I just get another set of eyes on the paperwork, I’ll see it’s only missed payments or normal foreclosure steps. But that’s kind of why I’m here — because no amount of papers they show me really changes what I’ve been seeing. For almost two years, it keeps coming back to the same pattern: the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nI’m not saying I wouldn’t look at an independent review. I would try. I just… I don’t think paperwork alone would change what I know is happening. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any evidence that would make you change your mind about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I want to say yes, because I know that’s probably the right answer… like, I don’t want to seem unreasonable.\n\nBut honestly, I don’t know what evidence would change my mind at this point. Everyone keeps saying it’s just paperwork, missed payments, normal foreclosure stuff, and they show me papers like that should settle it. But that doesn’t change what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nIt’s the same pattern over and over — the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job somehow lining up in a way that feels like they’re quietly working together to push me out of my house. So… I guess if there were some way to really prove they weren’t connected, maybe. I just don’t know what that would look like, because the paperwork hasn’t done that for me. Does that sound bad?\n\nDoctor: Was there anything unusual or particularly stressful happening in your life around the time you first started noticing these signs of the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I guess the main thing was the foreclosure notices starting, and then the bank calls… That’s when it really started taking over our home life.\n\nIt’s been about a year and nine months now, I think. I don’t want to make it sound like everything changed overnight, but around then is when I first started noticing the signs and feeling like it wasn’t just normal paperwork anymore. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or hobbies you've had to stop or reduce because of your concerns about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job?\n\nPatient: I mean… not completely, I don’t think. I still get up and go to work. I’m still supervising my team, doing schedules, dealing with customer issues, all of that. And I’m taking care of myself, so it’s not like I’ve just stopped functioning or anything.\n\nIt’s more that things get harder when something reminds me of the house situation — like mail coming in, calls from the bank, or certain paperwork at work that feels connected to all of it. Then I can get pulled into it, and I guess that probably crowds out other things I might normally do.\n\nI don’t want to overstate it, though. Day to day, if the foreclosure stuff isn’t right in front of me, I can still do what I need to do. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your beliefs about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you could be wrong?\n\nPatient: I mean… I know I’m supposed to say it could be wrong. I don’t want to sound stubborn or like I’m not listening.\n\nBut honestly, no, I don’t really think it’s wrong. I’m here because everyone keeps treating this foreclosure like it’s just missed payments and paperwork, and they keep showing me documents like that should make me drop it. But I know what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nThe bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job… it’s like they’ve been quietly working together to push me out of my house. I wish I could just believe it was all separate, normal things, but I can’t. Does that make sense, or does that sound too extreme?\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could happen or any information you could receive that would make you completely change your mind about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I want to be open to that, because I don’t want you to think I’m just refusing to listen. If there were something real that explained all of it — not just more paperwork — I’d try to look at it.\n\nBut… honestly, I don’t know what would make me completely change my mind. Everyone keeps acting like this foreclosure is just missed payments and forms, and they keep showing me documents like that should settle it. But I know what I’ve been seeing. The bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nSo I guess… no, not completely. Not unless something explained why all of those things have lined up the way they have for almost two years. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: How are you feeling emotionally when you think about the bank, loan servicer, and people at your job working together against you?\n\nPatient: I guess… I feel upset, mostly. I try not to let it get to me, because I know I still have to work and act normal, but when I really think about it, it’s stressful.\n\nIt makes me feel kind of on edge, like I have to keep watching what’s happening so I don’t miss something. And I feel hurt too, especially with the people at work being involved, because I don’t want to believe people around me would do that.\n\nI don’t know if that’s the kind of answer you mean. I’m not falling apart every minute, but it does weigh on me.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like things you see or hear in your daily life are specifically related to you or your situation, even if they don't seem directly connected to the bank, loan servicer, or your job?\n\nPatient: I mean… not really outside of all that, I don’t think. Or at least I try not to make everything about me, because I know how that can sound.\n\nBut sometimes when something happens during the day, even if it’s not directly from the bank or the loan servicer, I can’t help noticing how it lines up with the foreclosure situation. Like it feels connected, even if other people would probably say it’s just separate stuff.\n\nI’m here because everyone keeps acting like this is just paperwork and missed payments, but that’s not what it feels like to me. I know the bank, the loan servicer, and even people at my job have been quietly working together to push me out of my house. And honestly, no amount of papers they show me really changes what I’ve been seeing for almost two years.\n\nI hope that doesn’t sound like I’m trying to make everything into something. It’s just hard not to notice patterns when they keep happening.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Not usually TV or radio, no. I don’t sit there thinking every news story is about me or anything like that.

It’s more… when they talk about foreclosures or banks or loan servicers, I notice it, because that’s what’s happening to me. And I know how this sounds, so I’m trying to answer carefully. But I’m here because everyone keeps saying mine is just paperwork and missed payments, and I don’t think that’s true. But that’s kind of why I’m here — because no amount of papers they show me really changes what I’ve been seeing. For almost two years, it keeps coming back to the same pattern: the bank, the loan servicer, and even some people at my job quietly working together to push me out of my house.\n\nI’m not saying I wouldn’t look at an independent review.
